be the natural homomorphism from the simply connected cover of
\mathbf{G}^{der}
.
For any subgroup
H\subset\mathbf{G}(F)
set
H^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}=H\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
.

2.9.
A double Tits system

Set
H_{1}=G_{1}\cap H
for any subgroup
H\subset G
.
A good part of the following two sections relies heavily on the following theorem of Bruhat and Tits:

Theorem 2.9.1

The homomorphism
\nu_{N}
induces an isomorphism of groups
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
.
If
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
is the subset corresponding to
\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{a})
, then
the quadruple

(\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},I_{\mathfrak{a}},\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},\mathcal{S})

is a double Tits system whose Weyl group is
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and the injection
\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\to\mathbf{G}(F)
is
I_{\mathfrak{a}}
-
\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
-adapted of connected type.

Proof.

This is
[
BT84
, Proposition 5.2.12]
together with the fact that
I_{\mathfrak{a}}\cap\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}=\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
proved in Lemma
2.8.11
.
∎

The nomenclature ”double Tits system”
[
BT72
, §1.2.6]
means that both

(\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},I_{\mathfrak{a}},\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}})\text{ and }(\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},\mathbf{B}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}})

are Tits systems.
The second assertion means that
[
BT72
, §1.2.13]
: for any
g\in\mathbf{G}(F)
, there exists
h\in\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
such that
hI_{\mathfrak{a}}h^{-1}=gI_{\mathfrak{a}}g^{-1}
and
h\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}h^{-1}=g\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}g^{-1}
.
We refer the reader to the section
[
BT72
, §5.1]
for more details, especially Theorem 5.1.3 in
loc. cit.
for equivalent properties.
Being of connected type (Definition 4.1.3 in
loc. cit.
) shows that the images of
\nu(\mathbf{N}(F))
and
\nu(\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}})
in
GL(V)
are equal, i.e.
\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq W=\mathbf{N}(F)/\mathbf{M}(F)
.
Thanks to Theorem
2.9.1
we have:

Corollary 2.9.2

(1)

If
a\in\mathcal{A}
is a special point, then by the claim below
[
BT72
, Definition 4.1.3]
, we have
\mathbf{N}(F)\cap P_{a}=(\mathbf{N}(F)\cap K_{a})\cdot\ker\nu=(\mathbf{N}(F)\cap U_{a})\cdot\ker\nu
, thus
P_{a}=\ker\nu\,\cdot U_{a}=\ker\nu\,\cdot K_{a}
.

(2)

For any subset
\Upomega\subset\mathcal{A}
, set
P_{\Upomega}^{\dagger}
for its
\mathbf{G}(F)
-stabilizer, i.e.
\{g\in\mathbf{G}(F)\colon g\cdot\Upomega=\Upomega\}
and
N_{\Upomega}^{\dagger}=P_{\Upomega}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{N}(F)
.
We have

P_{\Upomega}^{\dagger}=N_{\Upomega}^{\dagger}K_{\Upomega}
.

Proof.

See
[
BT72
, §4.1]
for these statement.
∎

Corollary 2.9.3

For any bounded subset
\Upomega\subset\mathcal{A}
, we have

\widetilde{K}_{\Upomega}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\mathbb{P}_{\iota(\Upomega\times V_{G})}(\mathcal{O}_{F})\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}\text{ and }K_{\Upomega}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}.

Proof.

The equality
\widetilde{K}_{\Upomega}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}
is clear by double inclusions, since
\mathbf{M}(F)
acts by translations, so any element of
\mathbf{M}(F)
fixing any point fixes all of
\mathcal{A}_{ext}
, i.e. is in
\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}
.
The last equality
K_{\Upomega}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
follows readily from the first one using Lemma
2.8.11
.

The last equality, can also be proved using
[
HR10
, Lemma 4.1.1]
, which shows that for any facet
\mathcal{F}
in
\mathcal{A}_{ext}
the subgroup
\mathbf{M}(F)\cap K_{\mathcal{F}}
is a parahoric subgroup of
\mathbf{M}(F)
and hence must be
\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
by Corollary
2.8.7
.
∎

Lemma 2.9.4

For any special point
a\in\mathcal{A}
, the canonical injection
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{N}(F)\cap K_{a}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Because
a
is special, we know by Remark
2.3.7
that for any
\alpha\in\Phi_{red}
there exists
m_{\alpha}\in U_{a}\cap\mathbf{N}(F)

whose image in
W=\langle s_{\alpha}\colon\alpha\in\Phi_{red}\rangle
is the reflection
s_{\alpha}
.

∎

Lemma 2.9.5

Let
{\mathfrak{b}}\subset\mathcal{A}
be an alcove.
The subgroup
{P}_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}=N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cdot I_{\mathfrak{b}}
is the
\mathbf{G}(F)
-normalizer of
P_{\mathfrak{b}}
,
\widetilde{I}_{\mathfrak{b}}
and
I_{\mathfrak{b}}
. Moreover,

N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}=\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\text{ and }P_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\ker\nu.

Proof.

The statement on the normalizer is clear.
Since
P_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}
equals the
\mathbf{G}(F)^{{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}
-stabilizer of
{\mathfrak{b}}
which is the
\mathbf{G}(F)^{{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}
-normalizer of
I
, but this latter is its own normalizer (in
\mathbf{G}(F)^{{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}
), hence
P_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}=I_{\mathfrak{b}}
.
Intersecting this equality with
\mathbf{N}(F)
shows the first equality thanks to Lemma
2.8.11
.

Finally, an
m\in{P}_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)
acts by translation, and we have
\cup_{k\in\mathds{Z}_{\geq 0}}{\mathfrak{b}}+k\nu(m)\subset{\mathfrak{b}}
.
But since
{\mathfrak{b}}
is bounded, this inclusion can only happen if
\nu(m)=0
, i.e.
{P}_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\ker\nu
.
We can argue also using
\mathbf{M}(F)=\ker\nu\,\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
(by Corollary
2.9.6
), so
{P}_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)=\ker\nu\,({P}_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}})
which equals
\ker\nu
by the first part of this Lemma.
∎

Corollary 2.9.6

We have
\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\simeq\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and

\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\simeq W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq(\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1})\rtimes W.

Proof.

Given the isomorphism
\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
(Theorem
2.9.1
),
\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
is precisely the subgroup of elements in
\mathbf{N}(F)
that act on
\mathcal{A}
by translation, hence the map
\nu
induces an isomorphism

\mathbf{M}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}.

Accordingly, the isomorphism
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\rtimes W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{a}
for any special point
a\in\mathcal{A}
(§
2.5
) corresponds to the decomposition

\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}=(\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\cap\mathbf{M}(F))/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\rtimes(\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\cap K_{a})/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}.

This concludes the proof thanks to Lemma
2.9.4
.

∎

Corollary 2.9.7

Let
{\mathfrak{b}}\subset\mathcal{A}
be an alcove.
We have,

\mathbf{G}(F)=N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cdot\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and
\mathbf{N}(F)=N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}\cdot\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and
\mathbf{G}(F)/\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq\mathbf{N}(F)/\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{{\dagger}}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
.

Proof.

A immediate consequence of Theorem
2.9.1
is
\mathbf{G}(F)\cdot{\mathfrak{b}}=\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\cdot{\mathfrak{b}}
and so
\mathbf{G}(F)=P_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
, which proves the first equality given that
P_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}=N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}\cdot I_{\mathfrak{b}}
.
Intersecting this with
\mathbf{N}(F)
yields the second equality of the corollary.

This shows that the natural maps
N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}\to\mathbf{G}(F)/\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and
N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}\to\mathbf{N}(F)/\mathbf{N}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
are both surjective and have the same kernel
\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
by Lemma
2.9.5
.
∎

2.10.
The subgroup
\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}

Proposition
2.8.6
shows that
\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\subset\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}
and as we will see in the following Lemma, this is actually an equality:

Lemma 2.10.1

We have,
\mathbf{G}(F)^{1}=(N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{1})\cdot\varphi(\mathbf{G}_{sc}(F))\text{ and }\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}=\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}=\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}\cdot\varphi(\mathbf{G}_{sc}(F))
.

Proof.

The equality
\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}=\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
was already proved in
[
Ric16
, Lemma 1.3]
.
However, we present here another argument:
Intersecting
\mathbf{G}(F)=N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
of Corollary
2.9.7
with the kernel of
\nu_{G}
and
\kappa_{G}
yields

\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}=(N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)_{1})\cdot\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\text{ and }\mathbf{G}(F)^{1}=(N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{1})\cdot\mathbf{G}(F)^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}.

By Lemma
2.9.5
for
\mathbf{G}_{F^{un}}
(or by
[
HR08
, Lemma 17]
), we have

P_{\iota(\mathfrak{a})}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{G}(F^{un})^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}=P_{\iota(\mathfrak{a})}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{G}(F^{un})_{1}=\mathbb{P}_{\iota(\mathfrak{a}\times V_{G})}^{\circ}(\mathcal{O}_{F^{un}}),

where
P_{\iota(\mathfrak{a})}^{\dagger}
is the
\mathbf{G}(F^{un})
-stabilizer of
{\iota(\mathfrak{a})}
.
Now since clearly
P_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\subset P_{\iota(\mathfrak{a})}^{{\dagger}}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)
, we have

I_{\mathfrak{a}}\subset P_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}=P_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F^{un})_{1}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)\subset\mathbb{P}_{\iota(\mathfrak{a}\times V_{G})}^{\circ}(\mathcal{O}_{F^{un}})\cap\mathbf{G}(F)=I_{\mathfrak{a}}.

In conclusion, this shows
I_{\mathfrak{a}}=P_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}
and so
\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}=N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}\cap\mathbf{G}(F)_{1}
by Lemma
2.8.11
.
Finally, recall that the last one was already seen in §
2.8.7
, which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

2.11.
More on
\mathbf{G}(F)^{1}
-fixators

Lemma 2.11.1

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
be a facet containing in its closure a special point
a
, i.e.
{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\subset K_{a}
. We have

\widetilde{K}_{\mathcal{F}}=\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}K_{\mathcal{F}}\text{ and }\widetilde{K}_{\mathcal{F}}/{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}.

Proof.

This Lemma generalizes
[
Ros15
, Lemma 4.2.1]
and
[
HR10
, Proposition 11.1.4]
.
Here we give a different argument:
By Corollary
2.9.2
, one has
P_{a}=K_{a}\cdot\ker\nu
, hence

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=((K_{a}\cdot\,\ker\nu)\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{1})\cap P_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle(\text{Prop. \ref{parahr}})

\displaystyle=(K_{a}\cdot(\ker\nu\cap\mathbf{G}(F)^{1}))\cap P_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle(K_{a}\subset\mathbf{G}(F)^{1})

\displaystyle=(K_{a}\cdot\mathbf{M}(F)^{1})\cap P_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=(K_{a}\cap P_{\mathcal{F}})\cdot\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}\subset P_{\mathcal{F}})

\displaystyle=K_{\mathcal{F}}\cdot\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}.

Consequently, Lemma
2.9.3
yields
\widetilde{K}_{\Upomega}/{K}_{\Upomega}\simeq\mathbf{M}(F)^{1}/\mathbf{M}(F)_{1}
.
∎

Remark 2.11.2
.

By Lemma
2.8.11
we see that
M^{1}\hookrightarrow G^{1}
induces an inclusion
(\Lambda_{M})_{\text{tor}}\hookrightarrow(\Lambda_{G})_{\text{tor}}

compatible with
\kappa_{M}
and
\kappa_{G}
.
We see then:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda_{G})_{\text{tor}}=0}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{1}=G_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda_{M})_{\text{tor}}=0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{1}=M_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{K}_{\mathcal{F}}=K_{\mathcal{F}}.}

where
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{G},F)_{red}
is any facet containing a special point in its closure. The group
\Lambda_{M}
(which plays the role of the cocharacters lattice in the split case §
4
.) has no torsion:

•

If
\mathbf{M}
is a torus that splits over an unramified extension of
F
, i.e.
\mathbf{G}
is unramified
[
Bor79
, 9.5]
.

•

If
\mathbf{G}
is semisimple and simply connected then by
[
BT84
, §4.6.32 and §5.2.9]
one has
\widetilde{K}_{\mathcal{F}}=K_{\mathcal{F}}
,

•

See
[
HR10
, Corollary 11.1.5]
for other situations for which
\Lambda_{M}
is torsion free.

3.
Preliminary results around (Iwahori)–Hecke algebras

Notations

In addition to the notation fixed/adopted previously we need to introduce the following, that we hope will lighten a bit the exposition: For any algebraic
F
-groups
\mathbf{H}
(bold style), we denote its group of
F
-points by the ordinary capital letter
H=\mathbf{H}(F)
.
For any subgroup
X\subset G
different from
M
set
X_{1}:=X\cap G_{1}
. Recall that Lemma
2.10.1
says that
G_{1}=G^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
, so for any such a
X
, we have
X_{1}=X^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
.
For
M
, one might have
M_{1}=\ker\kappa_{M}\neq M\cap G_{1}
, for this reason, we keep the notation
M^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
for
M\cap G_{1}
.

Set
K:=K_{a_{\circ}}
for the special maximal parahoric subgroup attached to the special vertex
a_{\circ}\in\mathcal{A}
and
I:=I_{\mathfrak{a}}
be the Iwahori subgroup corresponding to the fixed alcove
{\mathfrak{a}}\subset\mathcal{A}
.

Recall that by Lemma
2.9.4
, the canonical injection
N\cap K/M\cap K\to W
is an isomorphism. Therefore, we may and will assume from now on, that any representative in
N
of a class in
W=N/M
lies in
K
.

3.1.
\flat
is everywhere !

Let
M_{1}\subset M^{\flat}\subset M
be any open compact subgroup, by maximality of
M^{1}

[
Lan96
, Proposition 1.2]
we must have
M^{\flat}\subset M^{1}
.

Lemma 3.1.1

The group
N
acts trivially on
\ker\nu/M_{1}
. In particular,
M^{\flat}
is normal in
N
.

Proof.

Let
n\in N
and
m\in\ker\nu
.
On the one hand, we clearly have
m^{-1}nmn^{-1}\in\ker\nu_{N}=\ker\nu
.
On the other hand, the commutator element
m^{-1}nmn^{-1}\in G_{1}
.
Therefore, Lemma
2.9.5
implies
m^{-1}nmn^{-1}\in\ker\nu\cap G_{1}=M_{1}
and accordingly
mM_{1}=nmn^{-1}M_{1}
.
∎

For any
X\subset G
, set
X^{\flat}=M^{\flat}X
. This procedure does not alter the structure of the object we are interested in, e.g. if
K_{\mathcal{F}}
is a parahoric subgroup attached to a facet
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
, then
K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
is still a group.

When
M^{\flat}=M_{1}
, the resulting objects will sometimes be described as ”parahoric” and the supscript
{\flat}
will be simply omitted, e.g.
I^{\flat}=I
the Iwahori subgroup,
K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=K_{\mathcal{F}}
the parahoric subgroup attached to a facet
\mathcal{F}
, and so on …
When
M^{\flat}=M^{1}
, the resulting objects will be described as ”geometric” and
{\flat}
will be replaced simply by
1
, e.g.
I^{1}=\widetilde{I},K^{1}=\widetilde{K}
.

Thanks to Lemma
2.8.11
, we have an isomorphism of groups
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}:=N_{1}^{\flat}/M^{\flat}\simeq W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
.
Recall that
\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{a})=\cup_{\mathcal{F}\text{ facet of }{\mathfrak{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
is the set of reflections at the walls of
\mathfrak{a}
and it generates
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
.
Here,

r
is the number of irreducible components of the Dynkin diagram of
\Phi
.
For any facet
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathfrak{a}
, let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\subset\mathcal{S}^{\flat}\subset W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
be the sets corresponding to
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\subset\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{a})\subset W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
.
Similarly, we put
\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}:=M^{\flat}M^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}/M^{\flat}\simeq\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and
we define for any facet
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
, the group
{W}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}:=N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}/M^{\flat}\simeq{W}_{\mathcal{F}}
where
N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}:=N\cap K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=M^{\flat}N_{1,\mathcal{F}}
.
The group
{W}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
is generated by
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
.
For example,
W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}
is canonically isomorphic to
W
.
Given Lemma
2.9.6
, we have

W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}=\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}.

Corollary 3.1.2

The quadruple
(G_{1}^{\flat},I^{\flat},N_{1}^{\flat},\mathcal{S}^{\flat})
is a double Tits system whose Weyl group is isomorphic to
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and the injection
G_{1}^{\flat}\hookrightarrow G
is
I_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\flat}
-
N_{1}^{\flat}
-adapted of connected type.

Proof.

This follows readily from Theorem
2.9.1
.
∎

Consequently, the pair
(W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat},\mathcal{S}^{\flat})
is a Coxeter system
[
Bou68
, §3]

subject to the following properties:

T_{1}.

The subgroup
N_{1}^{\flat}\cap I^{\flat}=M^{\flat}
is normal in
N_{1}^{\flat}
and
G_{1}^{\flat}
is generated by
I^{\flat}
and
N_{1}^{\flat}
,

T_{2}.

the elements of
\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
have order 2 and generate
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
,

T_{3}.

For all
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat},w\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
, we have
sI^{\flat}w\subset I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}\sqcup I^{\flat}swI^{\flat}
,

T_{4}.

For all
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
, we have
sI^{\flat}s\neq I^{\flat}
.

Corollary 3.1.3

(1)

G_{1}^{\flat}=\sqcup_{w\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}}I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}
.

(2)

For any facet
\mathcal{F}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{a}}
, the subgroup
K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=I^{\flat}W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}I^{\flat}
is its own normalizer in
G_{1}^{\flat}
.

(3)

For any two facet
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{a}}
, and
w\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
, we have
K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}wK_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}=I^{\flat}W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}wW_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}I^{\flat}
and the map
w\mapsto I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}
defines a bijection

W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}/W_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}\cong K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash G_{1}^{\flat}/K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}.

Proof.

These facts are consequences of the axioms
T_{1},\dots,T_{4}
, for which proofs may be found in
[
Bou68
, Ch. IV, §2 n
∘
5 & 6]
since for any
\mathcal{F}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{a}}
we know that
W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
is precisely the subgroup of
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
generated by
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
, the type of
\mathcal{F}
.
∎

Corollary 3.1.4

(Iwahori factorizations)

Let
{\mathfrak{b}}\subset\mathcal{A}
be an alcove.
The map
U_{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}\times U_{\mathfrak{b}}^{-}\times M^{\flat}\to I_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\flat}
is a bijection.
In particular,
I_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\flat}
admits the Iwahori factorization
I_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\flat}=U_{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}U_{\mathfrak{b}}^{-}M^{\flat}
in which the factors commute.
For
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{a}
, the product maps
\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\cap\Phi_{red}}U_{\alpha+0}\to U_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}=:I^{+}\text{ and }\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^{-}\cap\Phi_{red}}U_{\alpha+n_{\alpha}^{-1}}\to U_{\mathfrak{a}}^{-}=:I^{-}
are homeomeprisms.
Similarly, the product maps
\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^{\pm}\cap\Phi_{red}}U_{\alpha+0}\to U_{a_{\circ}}^{\pm}=K\cap U^{\pm}
are isomorphisms and
K^{\flat}=U_{a_{\circ}}^{+}U_{a_{\circ}}^{-}\,U_{a_{\circ}}^{+}M^{\flat}
.

Proof.

The corollary is a consequence of Proposition
2.8.9
, Lemma
2.8.11
,
4
of Proposition
2.4.5
together with Example
2.4.6
for the values of
f_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in\Phi_{red}
.
∎

3.2.
Dominance in
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}

Let
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}:=M/M^{\flat}
. Recall that we have a canonical isomorphism (see §
2.8.2
)

\Lambda_{M}=M/M_{1}\simeq\kappa_{M}(M)=(X^{*}(Z(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}))_{\text{In}})^{\langle\sigma\rangle}.

This shows that
\Lambda_{M}
is a finitely generated abelian group with finite torsion subgroup
(\Lambda_{M})_{\text{tor}}=M^{1}/M_{1}
, which injects into the set of permutations of the vertices of
\mathfrak{a}
.
So
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
is finitely generated abelian group with torsion subgroup
M^{1}/M^{\flat}
. Set
q_{tor}^{\flat}:=|M^{1}/M^{\flat}|
.

There exists a natural injective finite-cokernel homomorphism
X_{*}(\mathbf{S})\hookrightarrow X^{*}(Z(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}))_{\text{In}}^{\sigma}

[
Ros15
, §2.7]
, which yields an isomorphisms

X_{*}(\mathbf{S})\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}\simeq X^{*}(Z(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}))_{\text{In}}^{\sigma}\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}\simeq\Lambda_{M}\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}.

The map
\nu_{M}\colon M\to X_{*}(\mathbf{S})\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}
(Lemma
2.2.1
) identifies
\Lambda_{M}^{1}=M/M^{1}(=\Lambda_{M}/(\Lambda_{M})_{\text{tor}})
with a lattice
\underline{\Lambda}_{M}
in
X_{*}(\mathbf{S})\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}
and
\text{rank}({\Lambda_{M}/(\Lambda_{M})_{\text{tor}}})=\dim_{\mathds{R}}X_{*}(\mathbf{S})\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}
.
By the above isomorphisms, the extended apartment
\mathcal{A}_{ext}
acquires the structure of an affine space over
\Lambda_{M}\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}\simeq\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}\simeq\underline{\Lambda}_{M}\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}
, thus one gets an embedding

\underline{\Lambda}_{M}\to\mathcal{A}_{ext},\quad v\mapsto\nu_{M}(v)+(a_{\circ},0_{V_{G}}).

Define

\displaystyle M^{\pm}

\displaystyle:=\{m\in M\colon(\alpha+0)(\nu(m)+a_{\circ})=\langle\nu_{M}(m),\alpha\rangle\geq 0,\,\,\forall\alpha\in\Phi_{red}^{\pm}\}.

We call
M^{+}
(resp.
M^{-}
) the set of dominant (resp. antidominant) elements of
M
.
Let
{\Lambda}_{M}^{\pm,\flat}\subset{\Lambda}_{M}^{\flat}
and
\underline{\Lambda}_{M}^{\pm}\subset\underline{\Lambda}_{M}
denote the images of
M^{\pm,\flat}
by the natural projections
M\twoheadrightarrow{\Lambda}_{M}^{\pm,\flat}\twoheadrightarrow\underline{\Lambda}_{M}^{\pm}
.
In other words

\underline{\Lambda}_{M}^{\pm}:=\left((a_{\circ}+\underline{\Lambda}_{M})\cap\mathcal{C}^{\pm}\right)-a_{\circ}\subset\underline{\Lambda}_{M},

where
\mathcal{C}^{\pm}
are the two opposite vectorial chambers

\mathcal{C}^{\pm}:=\{a\in\mathcal{A}_{ext}\colon(\alpha+0)(a)=\alpha(a-a_{\circ})=\langle a-a_{\circ},\alpha\rangle\geq 0,\,\,\forall\alpha\in\Phi_{red}^{\pm}\}.

Remark 3.2.1
.

An element
m\in M
is in
M^{+}
if and only if
\nu_{N}(m)(a_{\circ})=\nu(m)+a_{\circ}\in\overline{\mathcal{C}}^{+}
(topological closure) and accordingly also
\nu_{N}(m)(\mathfrak{a})\subset\mathcal{C}^{+}
since by definition
\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathcal{C}^{+}
.

3.3.
Iwahori–Weyl group

Define the
\flat
-Iwahori–Weyl group for
G
to be

\widetilde{W}^{\flat}:=N/M^{\flat}.

Lemma
3.1.1
simply says that
\ker\nu/M_{1}\subset Z(\widetilde{W})
the center of
\widetilde{W}
.
We now present two semidirect product decompositions of the
\flat
-Iwahori–Weyl group.
These decompositions will be useful for studying the Iwahori–Hecke algebra.
Here is the first one:

Lemma 3.3.1

The
{\flat}
-Weyl–Iwahori subgroup has a natural structure of a quasi-Coxeter group

\widetilde{W}^{\flat}=W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}\simeq W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes\Lambda_{G}^{\flat},

where
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}:=N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}/M^{\flat}
, that is the subgroup of stabilizers of
\mathfrak{a}
in
\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
and
\Lambda_{G}^{\flat}:=G/G_{1}^{\flat}
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the short exact sequence
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{1}^{\flat}/M^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N/M^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N/N_{1}^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
which splits given that
N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}/M^{\flat}\simeq N/N_{1}^{\flat}\simeq G/G_{1}^{\flat}

(Corollary
2.9.7
).
∎

Lemma 3.3.2

The
{\flat}
-Iwahori–Weyl group
\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
admits the following decomposition

\widetilde{W}^{\flat}=\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\rtimes W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}\simeq\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\rtimes W.

If
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
is a facet, we have a bijection of
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
-sets

\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\cong\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\times(W/\jmath_{W}(W_{\mathcal{F}}))

where,
\jmath_{W}\colon N\to W=N/M
is the quotient map.

Proof.

Using the canonical isomorphism
W\simeq W_{a_{\circ}}
, proved in Lemma
2.9.4
, one gets a short exact sequence

\hbox to263.06pt{\vbox to19.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 131.53038pt\lower-9.52974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

which clearly splits, i.e.

\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\simeq\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\rtimes W
.

For the bijection statement, fix a set of representatives
D_{\mathcal{F}}=\{n_{{I^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{F}}\in N\colon 1\leq i\leq|W/\jmath_{W}(W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat})|\}
for
W/\jmath_{W}(W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat})
.
Note that
\jmath_{W}(W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat})=\jmath_{W}(W_{\mathcal{F}})
.
Given that
M\cap K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=M^{\flat}
(by Corollary
2.9.3
), one easily shows that for any
x\in N
there exist a unique pair
(m_{x},n_{x})\in\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\times D_{\mathcal{F}}
such that
xN_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=m_{x}n_{x}N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
. This completes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Remark 3.3.3
.

When
M^{\flat}=M^{1}=\nu_{N,ext}(\mathbf{N}(F))
,
{\widetilde{W}}^{1}
is called the extended affine Weyl group and usually denoted by
{\widetilde{W}}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
and it can be viewed as a group of affine-linear transformations on
\mathcal{A}_{ext}
marked with
(a_{\circ},0_{V_{G}})
as the ”origin”.

Remark 3.3.4
.

(i) For any
x\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
, we write
m_{x}
and
w_{x}
for its projection in
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
and
W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}
, respectively.

(ii) The natural projection morphism
\square^{\flat}\colon\widetilde{W}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
induces two isomorphisms;
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
and
W_{a_{\circ}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}

for which the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{M}\rtimes W_{a_{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\rtimes\widetilde{\Omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\rtimes W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{W}^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square^{\flat}\times\text{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Id}\times\square^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

In particular, the projection
\Lambda_{M}\to\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
is
W
-equivariant.

3.4.
Bruhat and Iwasawa decompositions

Let us recall Iwasawa and Bruhat decompositions for
G
:

Proposition 3.4.1

(The Bruhat and Iwasawa decompositions)

Let
K_{\mathcal{F}}
resp.
K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
be parahoric subgroups associated with facets
\mathcal{F}
resp.
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
contained in the apartment associated with
\mathbf{S}
.

G=U_{\mathcal{F}}NU_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\quad\text{(The Bruhat decomposition)},

G=U^{\pm}NB=U^{\pm}NU_{\mathcal{F}}\quad\text{(The Iwasawa decomposition)}.

Moreover, the maps
n\mapsto BnB
,
n\mapsto K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}nK_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
and
n\mapsto U^{\pm}nK_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
induce the following bijections

W\cong B\backslash G/B,\quad\,K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash G/K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}\cong{W}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/{W}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}\quad\text{ and }\quad U^{\pm}\backslash G/K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\cong\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}.

Proof.

•

By Corollary
2.9.7
and Corollary
3.1.3
we have
G=G_{1}N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}=I^{\flat}N_{1}^{\flat}I^{\flat}N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}
. But
N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}
normlaizes
I
(Lemma
2.9.5
) and hence

G=I^{\flat}N_{1}^{\flat}N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}I^{\flat}=U_{\mathfrak{a}}NU_{\mathfrak{a}}=U_{\mathcal{F}}NU_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}.

Accordingly, the map
\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\mapsto K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash G/K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
is surjective and factors through
{W}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/{W}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
.
Let
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
(with representatives
n,n^{\prime}\in N
) such that
K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}nK_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}=K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}n^{\prime}K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
.
Now
K_{\mathcal{F}}\subset G_{1}
, hence
\kappa_{G}(nM^{\flat})=\kappa_{G}(n^{\prime}M^{\flat})
so that
w=\tau\cdot w_{a}
and
\tau\cdot w_{a}^{\prime}
for a unique
\tau\in\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}
and
w_{a},w_{a}^{\prime}\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
, by Lemma
3.3.1
.
Consequently,
K_{\tau(\mathcal{F})}^{\flat}w_{a}K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}=K_{\tau(\mathcal{F})}^{\flat}w_{a}^{\prime}K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
with
\tau(\mathcal{F})\subset\overline{\mathfrak{a}}
, by definition of
\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}
. So by (2) Corollary
3.1.3
,
w_{a}=w_{a}^{\prime}
which ends the proof of the Bruhat decomposition and the bijection
K_{\mathcal{F}}\backslash G/K_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\simeq{W}_{\mathcal{F}}\backslash\widetilde{W}/{W}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
.

•

Let us prove the remaining statements.
Let
{\mathfrak{b}}
be an alcove that contains in its closure
\mathcal{F}
.
By
[
BT72
, Theorem 5.1.3 (vi)]
together with Theorem
2.9.1
, we have

G_{1}=B_{1}N_{1}I_{\mathfrak{b}}
.
Using Corollary
2.9.7
and Lemma
2.9.5
again we get

G=B_{1}N_{1}I_{\mathfrak{b}}N_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\dagger}=U^{+}NI_{\mathfrak{b}}=U^{+}NU_{\mathcal{F}}.

Assume
n,n^{\prime}\in N
with
n^{\prime}\in U^{+}nK_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=U^{+}K_{n\cdot\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}n=U^{+}U_{n\cdot\mathcal{F}}^{-}(N\cap K_{n\cdot\mathcal{F}}^{\flat})n
,
hence
n^{\prime}\in(N\cap K_{n\cdot\mathcal{F}}^{\flat})n=n(N\cap K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat})
(using
N\cap U^{+}U^{-}=\{1\}
).
This proves that
U^{+}\backslash G/K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\simeq N/N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
.
Finally, using the longest element
w_{0}\in W
, one obtains

G=U^{-}NU_{\mathcal{F}}
and this yields the corresponding bijection
U^{-}\backslash G/K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\simeq\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
.

•

For
G=\sqcup_{w\in W}BwB
we refer to
[
Mil17
, Theorem 21.73]
. ∎

Corollary 3.4.2

(Iwasawa decomposition II)

If
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{L}\ltimes\mathbf{U}_{P}^{+}
is any standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{G}
with Levi factor
\mathbf{L}
and unipotent radical
\mathbf{U}_{P}^{+}
, then
G=PK
, i.e.
K
is a good open compact subgroup of
G
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.4.1
, one has an Iwasawa decomposition
G=U^{+}NK=\cup_{w\in N/M}U^{+}MwK
. As observed in Lemma
2.9.4
, the classes of the quotient
W=N/M
admit representatives in
K
, therefore
G=B\cdot K=P\cdot K
. See the proof of Proposition
3.5.2
for the factorization
B\cap K=M_{1}(U^{+}\cap K)
.∎

Proposition 3.4.3

(Cartan decomposition for
K
)

The map
M\to K^{\flat}\backslash G/\penalty K^{\flat}
defined by
m\mapsto K^{\flat}mK^{\flat}
, induces
a bijection

\Lambda_{M}^{-,{\flat}}\cong W^{\flat}\backslash\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}\cong W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}\cong K^{\flat}\backslash G/\penalty K^{\flat}.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the Lemma for
M^{\flat}=M_{1}
. In which case, the first bijection is
[
HV15
, §6.3 Lemma]
, the second is a consequence of the decomposition of Lemma
3.3.2
and the third is given by Proposition
3.4.1
for
\mathcal{F}=\{a_{\circ}\}
.
∎

3.5.
Iwahori decompositions

We now give an Iwahori decomposition for the special parahoric subgroup
K
.
Note that we are no longer in the unramified case where we could have pulled up the Bruhat decomposition for the residue field of
F
.

Proposition 3.5.1

(Iwahori decomposition of
K
- I)

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a facet of the alcove
\mathfrak{a}
. We have the decomposition

K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=\bigsqcup_{w\in{W}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
G=\bigsqcup_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}}I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}
(Proposition
3.4.1
for
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{a}
).
∎

Proposition 3.5.2

(Iwahori decomposition of
K
- II)

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a facet of the alcove
\mathfrak{a}
. We have the decomposition

K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}U_{\mathcal{F}}^{+}wI^{\flat}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}U_{\mathcal{F}}^{-}wI^{\flat}.

Proof.

By the Iwahori decomposition
K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=U_{\mathcal{F}}^{+}\cdot U_{\mathcal{F}}^{-}\cdot N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
one shows

(U^{\pm}\cdot N)\cap K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=U_{\mathcal{F}}^{\pm}\cdot N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
.
Combining this equality with the decomposition
G=\sqcup_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}}U^{\pm}wI^{\flat}
(Proposition
3.4.1
), it becomes clear that

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}

\displaystyle=U_{\mathcal{F}}^{\pm}\cdot N_{1,\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\cdot I^{\flat}=\sqcup_{w\in W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}U_{\mathcal{F}}^{\pm}wI^{\flat}.\qed

3.6.
Conjugations

Lemma 3.6.1

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a facet of
\mathfrak{a}
that contains in its closure
a_{\circ}
. For any
n\in N_{1,a_{\circ}}
, we have

nU_{\mathcal{F}}^{-}n^{-1}\subset K_{\mathcal{F}}\text{ and }(nK_{\mathcal{F}}n^{-1})\cap U^{+}\subset I^{+}.

Proof.

Let
n\in N_{1,a_{\circ}}
, with image
w
in
W
. By Lemma
2.4.2
we have
nU_{\alpha+f_{\mathcal{F}}(\alpha)}n^{-1}=U_{\beta},
where,
\beta\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathds{R}
is the affine map
w(\alpha)+f_{\mathcal{F}}(\alpha)-w(\alpha)(\nu_{N}(n)(a_{\circ})-a_{\circ})
, thus
\beta=w(\alpha)+f_{\mathcal{F}}(\alpha)
since
n
fixes
a_{\circ}
. By Proposition
2.8.9
, we have for any fixed ordering
\prec
of
\Phi^{\pm}\cap\Phi_{red}

nU_{\mathcal{F}}^{\pm}n^{-1}=\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^{\pm}\cap\Phi_{red}}^{\prec}U_{w(\alpha)+f_{\mathcal{F}}(\alpha)}.

If
w(\alpha)\in\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{+}
then
U_{w(\alpha)+n_{\alpha}^{-1}}\subset U_{w(\alpha)+0}\subset K_{\mathcal{F}}
(Example
2.4.6
).
If
w(\alpha)\in\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{-}
, then
U_{w(\alpha)+n_{\alpha}^{-1}}=U_{w(\alpha)+n_{w(\alpha)}^{-1}}\subset K_{\mathcal{F}}
.
(Lemma
2.3.9
).
Therefore,
nU_{\mathcal{F}}^{-}n^{-1}\subset K_{\mathcal{F}}.

For the second equality, note that
(nK_{\mathcal{F}}n^{-1})\cap U^{+}=U_{\nu_{N}(n)(\mathcal{F})}^{+}\subset U_{a_{\circ}}^{+}=I^{+}
by Corollary
3.1.4
.
∎

Lemma 3.6.2

Let
{\Upomega}\subset\mathcal{A}
be any bounded subset and
\Psi
any closed subset of roots in
\Phi^{+}\cap\Phi_{red}
. For any
m\in M^{-}
, we have

m(U_{\Psi}\cap K_{\Upomega})m^{-1}\subset U_{\Psi}\cap K_{\Upomega}\text{ and }m^{-1}(U_{-\Psi}\cap K_{\Upomega})m\subset U_{-\Psi}\cap K_{\Upomega}.

In particular, if
\Psi=\Phi^{+}\cap\Phi_{red}
one gets
m^{-1}U_{\Upomega}^{-}m\subset U_{\Upomega}^{-}\text{ and }m\,U_{\Upomega}^{+}m^{-1}\subset U_{\Upomega}^{+}
.

Proof.

For any
r\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
, we have by Lemma
2.4.2

\displaystyle mU_{\alpha+r}m^{-1}

\displaystyle=U_{\alpha+r-\alpha(\nu_{N}(m)(a_{\circ})-a_{\circ})}=U_{\alpha+r-\langle\nu(m),\alpha\rangle}

and so, for all
r\in\Gamma_{\alpha}

mU_{\alpha+r}m^{-1}\begin{cases}\subset U_{\alpha+r}&\text{if }\langle\nu(m),\alpha\rangle\leq 0(\Leftarrow m\in M^{\pm}\text{ and }\alpha\in\Phi^{\mp}\cap\Phi_{red})\\
\supset U_{\alpha+r}&\text{if }\langle\nu(m),\alpha\rangle\geq 0(\Leftarrow m\in M^{\pm}\text{ and }\alpha\in\Phi^{\pm}\cap\Phi_{red})\end{cases}

By Proposition
2.8.9
, we have

m^{-1}U_{\Upomega}^{-}m=\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi^{-}\cap\Phi_{red}}^{\prec}U_{\alpha+f_{\Upomega}(\alpha)+\langle\nu(m),\alpha\rangle}.

for a fixed ordering
{\prec}
of the set
\Psi
.
Therefore, if
m
is antidominant:

\displaystyle m(U_{\Psi}\cap K_{\Upomega})m^{-1}\subset U_{\Psi}\cap K_{\Upomega},\quad m^{-1}U_{\Upomega}^{-}m\subset U_{\Upomega}^{-}\quad

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad m\,U_{\Upomega}^{+}m^{-1}\subset U_{\Upomega}^{+}.\qed

3.7.
Double cosets

Recall that
(W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{a}))
and
(W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat},\mathcal{S}^{\flat})
are isomorphic Coxeter systems.
Denote by
\ell^{\flat}\colon W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\to\mathds{N}
the length function of the Coxeter system. Inflate the map
\ell^{\flat}
to a length function
\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\simeq W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}\to\mathds{N}
, for which
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}
is exactly the subset of elements of length equals to
0
8
8

8

Equivalently, the length of an element in
w\in\widetilde{W}
is the number of walls between the fixed alcove
\mathfrak{a}
and the alcove
w(\mathfrak{a})
.
:

\ell^{\flat}(w)=\ell^{\flat}(w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}})\text{ if }w=w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}u\text{ with }w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat},u\in\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}.

Furthermore, we consider the Chevalley–Bruhat (partial) order
\overset{\flat}{\leq}

9
9

9

Let
(W,S)
be a Coxeter system and define a partial order on
W
as follows: Fix a reduced word
w=s_{1}s_{2}\dots s_{k}
. We say
v\leq w
if and only if there is a reduced subword
s_{i_{1}}s_{i_{2}}\dots s_{i_{j}}=v
such that
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\dots<i_{j}\leq k
.
on
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
.
Now extend this order to
\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
as follows: we say
(w_{1},u_{1})\overset{\flat}{\leq}(w_{2},u_{2})\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}
if and only if
w_{1}\overset{\flat}{\leq}w_{2}
and
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
.

Remark 3.7.1
.

Thanks to Remark
3.3.4
,
the projection
\square^{\flat}\colon\widetilde{W}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
preserves the length and the Chevalley–Bruhat order and we may and will write
\ell^{\flat}
(resp.
\overset{\flat}{\leq}
) simply as
\ell
(resp.
{\leq}
).

Remark 3.7.2
.

The Chevalley–Bruhat ordering of
(W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat},\mathcal{S}^{\flat})
can be defined in several ways, their equivalence is established in
[
Deo77
]
.
One interesting equivalent definition is: For
w,w^{\prime}\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
,
w\leq w^{\prime}
if there exists
\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{k}\in\Phi^{+}
such that
(i)
w^{\prime}=ws_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{k}}
and (ii)
ws_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{j-1}}(\alpha_{j})\in\Phi^{+}
for all
1\leq j\leq k
.

Lemma 3.7.3

For any
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
and
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
we have

I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}wI^{\flat}=\begin{cases}I^{\flat}swI^{\flat},&\text{ if }w<sw\quad(\ell(sw)=\ell(w)+1),\\
\hskip 0.0ptI^{\flat}wI^{\flat}\sqcup I^{\flat}swI^{\flat},&\text{ if }sw<w\quad(\ell(sw)=\ell(w)-1).\end{cases}

Proof.

Since the quadruplet
(G_{1}^{\flat},I^{\flat},N_{1}^{\flat},\mathcal{S}^{\flat})
is a Tits system, it verifies the axioms listed below Corollary
3.1.2
.
Therefore,
for any
w\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
and
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}

I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}wI^{\flat}=\begin{cases}I^{\flat}swI^{\flat},&\text{ if }w<sw,\\
\hskip 0.0ptI^{\flat}wI^{\flat}\sqcup I^{\flat}swI^{\flat},&\text{ if }sw<w.\end{cases}

To finish the proof, recall that elements of
\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}
are of zero length and their lifts in
N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}
normalizes
I^{\flat}
, therefore for any triplet
(s,w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},u)\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}\times W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\times\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}
, we have
I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}wI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}I^{\flat}u
, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.7.4
.

The first equality in the lemma above can be easily generalized: for any
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}

\displaystyle I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}w^{\prime}I^{\flat}

\displaystyle=I^{\flat}ww^{\prime}I^{\flat},\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\ell(ww^{\prime})=\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime}).\qed

Let us end this section with the following results, they will be needed later.
3.7.6
.

Corollary 3.7.5

Let
x,y\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
then

I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}yI^{\flat}\subset\bigsqcup_{z\leq y}I^{\flat}xzI^{\flat}\text{ and }I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}yI^{\flat}\subset\bigsqcup_{z\leq x}I^{\flat}zyI^{\flat}.

Proof.

One argues by induction on the size of a minimal word for
y
. Let
y=s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
, we have

I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}s_{1}I^{\flat}=\begin{cases}I^{\flat}xs_{1}I^{\flat},&\text{ if }x<xs_{1},\\
\hskip 0.0ptI^{\flat}xI^{\flat}\sqcup I^{\flat}xs_{1}I^{\flat},&\text{ if }xs_{1}<x.\end{cases}

So it suffices to take
z\in\{y\}
if
x<xs_{1}
and
z\in\{s_{1}y,y\}
if
xs_{1}<x
.
Let
y=s_{1}\cdots s_{r}
a reduced word for
y
. Put
y^{\prime}=\prod_{i=2}^{r}s_{{I^{\flat}}}
, we then have

\displaystyle I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}yI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}s_{1}y^{\prime}I^{\flat}

\displaystyle{=}I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}s_{1}I^{\flat}y^{\prime}I^{\flat}

\displaystyle\subset\begin{cases}\bigsqcup_{z\leq y^{\prime}}I^{\flat}xs_{1}zI^{\flat},&\text{ if }x<xs_{1},\\
\bigsqcup_{z\leq y^{\prime}}I^{\flat}xzI^{\flat}\cup\bigsqcup_{z\leq y^{\prime}}I^{\flat}xs_{1}zI^{\flat},&\text{ if }xs_{1}<x\end{cases}.

where the last inclusion is just the recursion hypothesis. Now if
z\leq y^{\prime}
then clearly
z<y
and
s_{1}z\leq y
, hence in both cases we have
I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}yI^{\flat}\subset\bigsqcup_{z\leq y}I^{\flat}xzI^{\flat}
for any
x\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
and
y\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
.

Now to extend this fact to any
y\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
, write
y=y_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}u\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}\rtimes\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}
. Because any lift of
u
in
N_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\dagger}
normalizes
I^{\flat}
one has

I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}y_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}uI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}xI^{\flat}y_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}I^{\flat}u\subset\bigsqcup_{z\leq y_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}I^{\flat}xzI^{\flat}u=\bigsqcup_{z\leq y_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}I^{\flat}xzuI^{\flat}\subset\bigsqcup_{z^{\prime}\leq y}I^{\flat}xz^{\prime}I^{\flat}.

This conclude the proof of the first inclusion, from which the second inclusion follows readily by upon applying the inverse map
w\mapsto w^{-1}
.
∎

Corollary 3.7.6

Let
x,y\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
. If
U^{+}x\cap I^{\flat}yI^{\flat}\neq\emptyset
then
x\leq y
in the Chevalley–Bruhat order.

Proof.

The proof resembles the proof of the claim in
[
HKP10
, Lemma 1.6.1]
.
Let
x,y\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
such that
U^{+}x\cap I^{\flat}yI^{\flat}\neq\emptyset
.
There exist then
u\in U^{+}
such that
ux\in I^{\flat}yI^{\flat}
.
Choose any
m\in M^{-}
such that
mum^{-1}\in U^{+}\cap I^{\flat}
.
Therefore,

I^{\flat}mxI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}muxI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}miyi^{\prime}I^{\flat}\subset I^{\flat}mI^{\flat}yI^{\flat}.

Given Corollary
3.7.5
, this shows

I^{\flat}mxI^{\flat}\subset\bigsqcup_{z\leq y}I^{\flat}mzI^{\flat}
,
and consequently
x\leq y
.
∎

3.8.
Volumes and
q
-powers

Lemma 3.8.1

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
be any facet.
For any
n\in N
, we have
q_{\mathcal{F},n}:=[{K}_{\mathcal{F}}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}:{K}_{\mathcal{F}}]=[{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}:{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}]
.

Proof.

This is a generalization of
[
Ros15
, Proposition 4.2.3]
, in which a similar statement is proved when
n\in W
,
M^{\flat}=M^{1}
and
\mathcal{F}=\mathfrak{a}
. Here, we present a different argument:

Using Lemma
3.1.1
, note that

{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=K_{\mathcal{F}}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
, the map
K_{\mathcal{F}}\to{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}/{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
, given by
x\mapsto xn{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
is then clearly surjective.
Two elements
x,y\in{K}_{\mathcal{F}}
give the same image if and only if
xn\in yn{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=\sqcup_{m\in M^{\flat}/M_{1}}ynm{K}_{\mathcal{F}}
, if and only if there exists
m\in M^{\flat}
such that
n^{-1}y^{-1}xn\in m{K}_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Given that
K_{\mathcal{F}}\subset\ker\kappa_{G}
, we see that

\kappa_{G}(m)=\kappa_{G}(n^{-1}y^{-1}xn)=1
, i.e.
m\in G_{1}\cap M^{1}=M_{1}
(Lemma
2.8.11
).
This shows that
{K}_{\mathcal{F}}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}/{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\cong{K}_{\mathcal{F}}/n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}n^{-1}\cap{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}n{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}/{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}.

∎

Lemma 3.8.2

Let
w=s_{1}\cdots s_{r}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
be a reduced word for
w
. Then,

q_{w}:=q_{\mathfrak{a},w}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}q_{\mathfrak{a},s_{i}}
. Moreover,
q_{w}
is a power of
q
for any
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
.

Proof.

Let
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
and
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
such that
\ell(sw)=\ell(w)+1
.
By Lemma
3.7.3
, we know that
I^{\flat}swI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\cdot I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}
.
By assumption,
s\neq w
, hence
I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\cap I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}\neq\emptyset
and accordingly
q_{sw}=q_{s}q_{w}
.
Therefore, the equality
q_{w}:=q_{\mathfrak{a},w}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}q_{\mathfrak{a},s_{i}}
follows by induction on the length
r
of
w
.
Consequently, to show that
q_{w}
is a power of
q
it suffices to show the claim for all elements of
\mathcal{S}
.
For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
(corresponding to the affine root
\alpha_{s}\in\Sigma_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
) we have, as in the proof of
[
Mac71
, Lemma 2.7.4]

\displaystyle IsI/I

\displaystyle\cong U_{\alpha_{s}}sI/I

[
Mac71
, Lemma 2.6.7 (1)]

\displaystyle\cong U_{\alpha_{s}}/U_{\alpha_{s}}\cap I_{\nu(s)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}

\displaystyle\cong U_{\alpha_{s}}/U_{\alpha_{s}+1}

[
Mac71
, Lemma 2.6.5]

\displaystyle.\qed

This completes the proof of the lemma since the size of the last quotient is a power of
q
.

Lemma 3.8.3

For any
n\in N
, we have a bijection

U^{+}I\cap nIn^{-1}I/I\cong\prod_{\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{+}}^{\prec_{+}}U_{\alpha+f_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)}/U_{\alpha+\max(0,f_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha))}

for any fixed ordering
\prec_{\pm}
of
\Phi^{\pm}
.
In particular,
|U^{+}I\cap nIn^{-1}I|_{I}
is a power of
q
.

Proof.

Write
nIn^{-1}=I_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}
.
Using Corollary
3.1.4
, we have

U^{+}I\cap I_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}I=(U^{+}M_{1}I^{-}\cap I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}})I=I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}I.

This equality yields the bijection
I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}I/I\cong I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}/I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}\cap I^{+}
.

By Corollary
3.1.4
again together with
4
and
5
of Proposition
2.4.5
, we have

I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}=\prod_{\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{+}}^{\prec_{+}}U_{\alpha+f_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)}\text{ and }I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}\cap I^{+}=\prod_{\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{+}}^{\prec_{+}}U_{\alpha+\max(0,f_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha))}

for any fixed ordering
\prec_{\pm}
of
\Phi^{\pm}
.
Therefore, we get a bijection

I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}I/I\cong\prod_{\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{+}}^{\prec_{+}}U_{\alpha+f_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)}/U_{\alpha+\max(0,f_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha))}.

This completes the proof of the lemma since the size of each quotient in the right product is a power of
q
.
∎

Corollary 3.8.4

Let
n=m\cdot w
for any
(m,w)\in\Lambda_{M}^{-}\times W_{a_{\circ}}
, we have
U^{+}I\cap nIn^{-1}I=I
.

Proof.

This corollary follows readily using Lemma
3.6.1
and Lemma
3.6.2
. We can also deduce it from Lemma
3.8.3
:
Let
n=m\cdot w
for any
m\in\Lambda_{M}^{-}
and any
w\in W_{a_{\circ}}
.
By Remark
2.4.6
, we have
f_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)=-\langle\nu(m),\alpha\rangle+f_{\mathfrak{a}}(w^{-1}(\alpha))
.
In particular, if
n\in\Lambda_{M}^{-}\cup W_{a_{\circ}}
, then

\langle\nu(m),\alpha\rangle\geq 0
for
\alpha\in\Phi_{red}\cap\Phi^{+}
and

n_{\alpha}^{-1}\leq f_{\mathfrak{a}}(w^{-1}(\alpha))\leq 0
then
I_{\nu_{N}(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}^{+}\subset I
and so
U^{+}I\cap I_{\nu(n)\cdot\mathfrak{a}}I=I
by Lemma
3.8.3
.
∎

3.9.
Double cosets decomposition

Lemma 3.9.1

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be two facets of
\mathfrak{a}
.
For any
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
we have

K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}wK_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}=I^{\flat}W_{\mathcal{F}}wW_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}I^{\flat}.

We will be interested later in the particular case
w=m\in\Lambda_{M}^{-}
and
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, for which we have
K_{\mathcal{F}}mK_{\mathcal{F}}=\sqcup_{(w_{1},w_{2})\in J_{m,\mathcal{F}}}Iw_{1}mw_{2}I
, where
J_{m,\mathcal{F}}\simeq W_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}\backslash W_{\mathcal{F}}\times W_{\mathcal{F}}
.
Here,
w\in W_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}
(the isotropy subgroup of
m
in
W_{\mathcal{F}}
) acts on
(w_{1},w_{2})\in W_{\mathcal{F}}\times W_{\mathcal{F}}
as follow
(w_{1}w,w^{-1}w_{2})
.

Proof.

For
w=m\in\Lambda_{M}^{-,\flat}
, the equality can easily be obtained by combining Proposition
3.4.1
and Lemma
3.6.2
.
However, here is an argument for a general
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
.
For any
s_{1},\cdots,s_{x}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
and
t_{1},\cdots,t_{y}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
,
Lemma
3.7.5
shows

I^{\flat}s_{1}\cdots s_{x}I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}t_{1}\cdots t_{y}I^{\flat}\subset I^{\flat}W_{\mathcal{F}}wW_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}I^{\flat}.

But
W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
(resp.
W_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
) is generated by
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
(resp.
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}
) and hence Lemma
3.5.1
yields

K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}wK_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\flat}\subset I^{\flat}W_{\mathcal{F}}wW_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}I^{\flat}
.
The opposite inclusion is obvious.
∎

3.10.
The modular function

Let
\delta_{B}
be the modular function on the fixed minimal parabolic
B
containing
M
given by the normalized absolute value of the determinant of the adjoint action on
\text{Lie}\,U^{+}
:

\delta_{B}(m):=|\det(\text{Ad}(m)_{\text{Lie}U^{+}})|_{F},\quad\forall m\in M

where
|\cdot|_{F}
is the fixed normalized absolute value of
F
.

Lemma 3.10.1

Let
\Upomega\subset\mathcal{A}_{ext}
be any bounded subset containing any alcove, then for any
m\in M^{-}

[K_{\Upomega}mK_{\Upomega}:K_{\Upomega}]=\delta_{B}(m)^{-1}.

In particular, if
\Upomega
is an alcove
\mathfrak{b}
, then
q_{m}=[I_{\mathfrak{b}}mI_{\mathfrak{b}}:I_{\mathfrak{b}}]=\delta_{B}(m)^{-1}
and so
q_{w(m)}=\delta_{B}(m)^{-1}
for all
m\in M^{-}
.

Proof.

Consider the map
i_{1}\mapsto i_{1}mK_{\Upomega}
from the set
K_{\Upomega}
to
K_{\Upomega}mK_{\Upomega}/K_{\Upomega}
. This yields a bijection

K_{\Upomega}mK_{\Upomega}/K_{\Upomega}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}K_{\Upomega}/K_{\Upomega}\cap mK_{\Upomega}m^{-1}.

Here,
K_{\Upomega}\cap mK_{\Upomega}m^{-1}=K_{\Upomega}\cap K_{\nu(m)(\Upomega)}
is the
K_{\Upomega_{m}^{\text{conv}}}={P}(F^{un})_{\Upomega_{m}^{\text{conv}}}^{\circ}\cap G_{1}
the
G_{1}
-fixator of the convex hull of
\Upomega_{m}^{\text{conv}}:=\Upomega\cup\nu(m)(\Upomega)
. Since
m\in M^{-}
, then
K_{\Upomega}\cap mK_{\Upomega}m^{-1}=U_{\Upomega}^{-}M_{1}mU_{\Upomega}^{+}m^{-1}
and so

\displaystyle[K_{\Upomega}:K_{\Upomega}\cap mK_{\Upomega}m^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[U_{\Upomega}^{+}:mU_{\Upomega}^{+}m^{-1}]=\delta_{B}^{-1}(m).

For the last statement, it suffices to observe that
q_{w(m)}=q_{\mathcal{F},w^{-1}(\mathfrak{a})}=\delta_{B}(m)^{-1}
for any
m\in M^{-}.
∎

Remark 3.10.2
.

A similar proof of
[
Mac71
, Proposition 3.2.15]
shows that for any
m\in\Lambda_{M}^{-}
we have

q_{a_{\circ},m}=[KmK:K]=\delta_{B}(m)^{-1}\frac{\sum_{w\in W_{a_{\circ}}}q_{w}^{-1}}{\sum_{w\in W_{a_{\circ}}^{m}}q_{w}^{-1}}

where,
W_{a_{\circ}}^{m}
is the isotropy group of
m
in
W_{a_{\circ}}
.

3.11.
Relative Hecke algebras

For any ring
R
, set
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,R)
for the
R
-module of locally constant and compactly supported functions
f\colon G\to R
. Let
H
be any open compact subgroup of
G
. The group
G
has a unique left invariant measure
\mu_{H}
normalized by
H
on
\mathds{Q}

[
Vig96
, §2.4]
:

\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})\longrightarrow\mathds{Q},\quad f\longmapsto\int_{G}f(g)d\mu_{H}(g)\text{ such that }\int_{G}{\bf 1}_{H}(g)d\mu_{H}(g)=1.

The vector space
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})
acquire the structure of a
\mathds{Q}
-algebra without a unit, when endowed with the convolution product with respect to
\mu_{H}
:

f*_{H}f^{\prime}\colon x\mapsto\int_{G}f(g)f^{\prime}(g^{-1}x)d\mu_{H}(g)\quad(f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})).

Remark 3.11.1
.

The above expression of the convolution can be rewritten

\displaystyle f*_{H}f^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{G}f(xg)f^{\prime}(g^{-1})d\mu_{H}(g).

This shows that for
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})
, if
f
is
X
-invariant on the left and
f^{\prime}
is
Y
-invariant on the right, then
f*_{H}f^{\prime}
is
X
-invariant on the left and
Y
-invariant on the right.

The group
G
acts on the underlying
\mathds{Q}
-vector space of this algebra by translation, on the left and on the right as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G\times G)\times\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((g,g^{\prime}),f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((g,g^{\prime})\cdot f\colon x\mapsto f(g^{-1}xg^{\prime})).}

Lemma 3.11.2

Let
X,Y
be two open compact subgroups of
G
. For any
g,h\in G
, one has

\displaystyle(i)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{gY}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{hX}

\displaystyle=|Y\cap hXh^{-1}|_{H}\mathbf{1}_{gYhX}

\displaystyle(ii)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{gY}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{XhX}

\displaystyle=\frac{|Y\cap X|_{H}}{|X\cap hXh^{-1}|_{H}}\mathbf{1}_{gYX}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{hX},

where, the notation
|\square|_{H}
denotes the volume of
\square
with respect to the measure
\mu_{H}
.

Proof.

(i) Observe that the function

\mathbf{1}_{gY}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{hX}(a)=\int_{G}\mathbf{1}_{gY}(b)\mathbf{1}_{hX}(b^{-1}a)d\mu_{H}(b),

can only be nonzero on the set
gYhX
. Let
a\in gYhX
and write it as
a=gyhx
, thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{gY}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{hX}(a)=|gY\cap gyhxXh^{-1}|_{H}=|Y\cap hXh^{-1}|_{H}

where, the third equality holds by left invariance of
\mu_{H}
.
For (ii), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{gY}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{XhX}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|X\cap hXh^{-1}|_{H}}(\mathbf{1}_{gY}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{X})*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{hX}=\frac{|Y\cap X|_{H}}{|X\cap hXh^{-1}|_{H}}\mathbf{1}_{gYX}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{hX}.\qed

Example 3.11.3
.

For
g,g^{\prime}\in G
, write
Hg^{\prime}H
as disjoint union
\sqcup_{h^{\prime}}h^{\prime}g^{\prime}H
, then by lemma above:

\mathbf{1}_{gH}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}=\mathbf{1}_{gHg^{\prime}H}=\mathbf{1}_{\sqcup_{h^{\prime}}gh^{\prime}g^{\prime}H}=\sum_{h^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{gh^{\prime}g^{\prime}H}.

If also
HgH=\sqcup_{h}hgH
, then

\mathbf{1}_{HgH}*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}=(\sum_{h}\mathbf{1}_{hgH})*_{H}\mathbf{1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}=\sum_{h,h^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{hgh^{\prime}g^{\prime}H}
.

Now, we associate to the pair
(G,H)
different
\mathds{Z}
-modules.

Definition 3.11.4
.

For any commutative ring
A
, we define
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,A)
to be the
A
-module of compactly supported functions
f\colon G\to A
that are
H
-invariant on the right.
It has the following canonical basis
\{{\bf 1}_{gH}\colon g\in G/H\}
.
In addition, the group left action of
G
on
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G,\mathds{Q})
as defined above, restricts to a left action on
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,\mathds{Z})
.
We also define
\mathcal{H}_{c}(G\sslash H,A)\subset\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,A)
to be the
A
-algebra
10
10

10

By Remark
3.11.1
and Example
3.11.3
, we see that
\mathcal{H}_{H}(A):=\mathcal{C}_{c}(G\sslash H,\mathds{Z})\subset\mathcal{C}_{c}(G\sslash H,\mathds{Q})
is stable under
*_{H}
.
of functions
f\colon G\to A
,
that are also
H
-invariant on the left.
We call
\mathcal{H}_{c}(G\sslash H,A)
the Hecke algebra relative to
H
with values in
A
and denote
\mathcal{H}_{H}(A)
.
We have
\mathcal{H}_{H}(A)=\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}A
.

The Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})
is a free
\mathds{Z}
-module, it has the canonical basis
\{{\bf 1}_{HgH}\colon g\in HgH\}
.

Example 3.11.5
.

According to Proposition
3.4.1
, for any facet
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
, the Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
has the following canonical
\mathds{Z}
-basis

\{{\bf 1}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}wK_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}\text{ for }w\in W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\}.

When
\mathcal{F}=\{a_{\circ}\}
,
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
will be called the
{\flat}
-special–Hecke algebra.
By Lemma
3.4.3
, one has
\Lambda_{M}^{-,{\flat}}\cong W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{\flat}/W_{a_{\circ}}^{\flat}
, so one can exhibit a natural
\mathds{Z}
-basis for
\mathcal{H}_{K^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
as follows
\{h_{m}^{\flat}:={\bf 1}_{K^{\flat}mK^{\flat}}\text{ for }m\in\Lambda_{M}^{-,{\flat}}\}
.
When
\mathcal{F}=\mathfrak{a}
,
\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
is called the
{\flat}
-Iwahori–Hecke algebra and the following set forms a
\mathds{Z}
-basis
\{i_{w}^{\flat}:={\bf 1}_{I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}}\text{ for }w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\}.

When we are in the case
M^{\flat}=M_{1}
, we systematically omit the prefix
{\flat}
in all of the above nomenclatures and notations.

Let
End_{\mathds{Z}[G]}\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,\mathds{Z})
denote the ring of
G
-equivariant endomorphisms of
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,\mathds{Z})
.

Proposition 3.11.6

The following map is an isomorphism of rings
11
11

11

the superscript
opp
indicates the opposite ring
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces End_{\mathds{Z}[G]}\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,\mathds{Z})^{\text{\emph{opp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{h}\colon f\mapsto f*_{H}h,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proof.

We first prove that the above map is an isomorphism of
\mathds{Z}
-modules. The injectivity being clear, we prove surjectivity. Any
G
-equivariant endomorphism
e
of
\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,\mathds{Z})
is uniquely defined by
e({\bf 1}_{H})
. Since
{\bf 1}_{H}
is
H
-invariant on the left,
e({\bf 1}_{H})
must also be
H
-invariant on the left. This shows that
e
is the image of the Hecke element
e({\bf 1}_{H})
.

Finally, for any
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})
, we clearly have

\displaystyle e_{h_{1}*_{H}h_{2}}({\bf 1}_{H})

\displaystyle=e_{h_{2}}\circ e_{h_{1}}({\bf 1}_{H})

As we previously said, elements of
End_{\mathds{Z}[G]}\mathcal{C}_{c}(G/H,\mathds{Z})
are uniquely determined by the image they give to
{\bf 1}_{H}
, this shows that
e_{h_{1}*_{H}h_{2}}=e_{h_{2}}\circ e_{h_{1}}
and ends the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark 3.11.7
.

The map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\vee}\colon g\mapsto h(g^{-1}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

is an involution and for any
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})
we have
(h_{1}*_{H}h_{2})^{\vee}=h_{2}^{\vee}*_{H}h_{1}^{\vee}
.

The following lemma is meant to clarify the multiplicative structure of the algebra
\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})
.

Lemma 3.11.8

For
g,g^{\prime}\in G
, we have

{\bf 1}_{HgH}*_{H}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}=\sum_{g^{\prime\prime}\in C_{g,g^{\prime}}}c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}){\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime\prime}H},\quad c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}):=\big|HgH\cap g^{\prime\prime}Hg^{\prime-1}H\big|_{H}

where,
C_{g,g^{\prime}}
denotes a set of representatives for
H\backslash HgHg^{\prime}H/H
.

Proof.

First, we know that the function
{\bf 1}_{HgH}*_{H}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}
is
H
-biinvariante (Remark
3.11.1
), therefore, using the canonical basis of
\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})
, it can be written as follows

{\bf 1}_{HgH}*_{H}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}=\sum_{g^{\prime\prime}\in C_{g,g^{\prime}}}c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}){\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime\prime}H},\quad c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})\in\mathds{Z}_{\geq 0}

for some finite set
C_{g,g^{\prime}}
and integral coefficients
c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})\neq 0
, for each
g^{\prime\prime}\in C_{g,g^{\prime}}
.

Secondly, the integral defining
{\bf 1}_{HgH}*_{H}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}(a)

is nonzero only if
a\in HgHg^{\prime}H
. Therefore, if
Hg^{\prime\prime}H\subset HgHg^{\prime}H
, we have
c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})={\bf 1}_{HgH}*_{H}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}(g^{\prime\prime})=\big|HgH\cap g^{\prime\prime}Hg^{\prime-1}H\big|_{H}\in\mathds{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
For each double coset
Hg^{\prime\prime}H\subset HgHg^{\prime}H
, write
g^{\prime\prime}=gh^{\prime\prime}g^{\prime}
for some
h^{\prime\prime}\in H
, thus

c^{H}(g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime})=\big|HgH\cap gh^{\prime\prime}g^{\prime}Hg^{\prime-1}H\big|_{H}\geq\big|gH\big|_{H}=1.

This shows that
C_{g,g^{\prime}}
is indeed a set of representatives for
H\backslash HgHg^{\prime}H/H
.
∎

Remark 3.11.9
.

For any
g,g^{\prime}\in G
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{G}{\bf 1}_{HgH}*_{H}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}(a)d\mu_{H}(a)

\displaystyle=\int_{G}\left(\int_{G}{\bf 1}_{Hg^{\prime}H}(b^{-1}a)d\mu_{H}(a)\right){\bf 1}_{HgH}(b)d\mu_{H}(b)

\displaystyle=\left|Hg^{\prime}H\right|_{H}\int_{G}{\bf 1}_{HgH}(b)d\mu_{H}(b)=\left|HgH\right|_{H}\left|Hg^{\prime}H\right|_{H}.

This shows that the linear functional
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{H}\colon\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{Z},}

defined on the canonical basis elements by
{\bf 1}_{HgH}\longmapsto\left|HgH\right|_{H}
, is an homomorphism of rings. Consequently,

{\bf 1}_{HgH}\in\mathcal{H}_{H}(\mathds{Z})^{\times}\Longleftrightarrow d_{H}\left({\bf 1}_{HgH}\right)=1\Longleftrightarrow g\in N_{G}(H).

Remark 3.11.10
.

Let
\Upomega\subset\mathcal{A}_{ext}
be any bounded subset containing the alcove
\mathfrak{a}
. Proposition
2.8.9
shows that the product map yields an isomorphism
U^{-}_{\Upomega}\times M_{1}\times U^{+}_{\Upomega}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}K_{\Upomega}
.

For any
m_{1},m_{2}\in M^{-}
, applying Lemma
3.6.2
shows that

K_{\Upomega}m_{1}K_{\Upomega}m_{2}K_{\Upomega}=K_{\Upomega}m_{1}m_{2}K_{\Upomega}
and
K_{\Upomega}m_{1}K_{\Upomega}\cap m_{1}m_{2}K_{\Upomega}m_{2}^{-1}K_{\Upomega}=m_{1}K_{\Upomega}
.
Accordingly, by Lemma
3.11.8
, we have

{\bf 1}_{K_{\Upomega}m_{1}K_{\Upomega}}*_{K_{\Upomega}}{\bf 1}_{K_{\Upomega}m_{2}K_{\Upomega}}={\bf 1}_{K_{\Upomega}m_{1}m_{2}K_{\Upomega}}.

Therefore, we get an homomorphism of rings
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{Z}[\Lambda_{M}^{-}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{K_{\Upomega}}(\mathds{Z})}
with commutative image.

Lemma 3.11.11

Let
H^{\prime}\subset H
be two open compact subgroups of
G
(e.g.
{H^{\prime}}=I
and
{H}=K
).
For any ring
R
in which
[H:H^{\prime}]
is invertible, we have a natural isomorphism of algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{H}_{H}(R),+,*_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(e_{H}*_{H^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{H^{\prime}}(R)*_{H^{\prime}}e_{H},+,*_{H^{\prime}}),\quad h}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|{H}|_{H^{\prime}}}^{-1}h.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
e_{H}:={|{H}|_{H^{\prime}}}^{-1}{\bf 1}_{H}
is an idempotent of the relative Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}_{H^{\prime}}(R)
.

Proof.

This is clear. ∎

3.12.
”Averaging” maps are homomorphisms

Lemma 3.12.1

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
be any facet.
For any
w\in N
, we have

{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}=\mathbf{1}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}wK_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{K_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}={\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}*_{K_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}wK_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathbf{1}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}=\sum_{m\in M^{\flat}/M_{1}}{\bf 1}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}wmK_{\mathcal{F}}}.

In particular,
\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}\in Z(\mathcal{H}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathds{Z}))
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.11.2
, one shows that for any
w\in N

|{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\cap w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w^{-1}|_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}=|{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\cap w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w^{-1}|_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}

Note that
{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\cap w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w^{-1}\subset G_{1}
, hence it equals
{K}_{\mathcal{F}}\cap w({K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}\cap G_{1})w^{-1}={K}_{\mathcal{F}}\cap w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w^{-1}
. So
\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}=\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}.

By Lemma
3.1.1
, we have
{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}={K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}
, hence

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle={\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}={\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}.

Finally, since
M^{\flat}
normalizes
K_{\mathcal{F}}
and
{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}=\sqcup_{m\in M^{\flat}/M_{1}}m{K}_{\mathcal{F}}
, one has

\sum_{m\in M^{\flat}/M_{1}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}wm{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}={\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}
.
∎

Corollary 3.12.2

The ”averaging” map
\iota^{\flat}\colon\mathcal{H}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathds{Z})\to\mathcal{H}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
defined on basis elements by

{\bf 1}_{K_{\mathcal{F}}wK_{\mathcal{F}}}\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}

is a surjective homomorphism of algebras.

Proof.

Clearly it is a surjective morphism of
\mathds{Z}
-modules.
It suffices then to verify that
\iota
respects the algebra structure.
For any two elements
w,w^{\prime}\in N
, Lemma
3.12.1
shows

\displaystyle\iota({\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w^{\prime}{K}_{\mathcal{F}}})

\displaystyle={\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w^{\prime}{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle={|M^{\flat}/M_{1}|}^{-1}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}w^{\prime}{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle={\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}*_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}{\bf 1}_{{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}w^{\prime}{K}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\flat}}\qed

3.13.
Iwahori–Matsumoto presentation and other properties

Recall that
\mathcal{S}\subset N_{1}/M_{1}
corresponds to the set of orthogonal reflections with respect to the walls of the fixed alcove
\mathfrak{a}
(Using the identification
N_{1}/M_{1}\simeq W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
).

Theorem 3.13.1

(The Iwahori–Matsumoto Presentation)

The Iwahori–Hecke ring
\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
is the free
\mathds{Z}
-module with basis
(i_{w}^{\flat})_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}}
endowed with the unique ring structure satisfying

•

The braid relations:

i_{w}^{\flat}*_{I^{\flat}}i_{w^{\prime}}^{\flat}=i_{ww^{\prime}}^{\flat}\text{ if }w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\text{ such that }\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})=\ell(ww^{\prime})
.

•

The quadratic relations:

(i_{s}^{\flat})^{2}=q_{s}i_{1}^{\flat}+(q_{s}-1)i_{s}^{\flat}\text{ if }s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
.

This is a very known result, at least for Iwahori–Hecke attached to extended affine Weyl groups. See for example
[
Gar97
, §6.3]
,
[
GP00
]
,
[
CR62
]
,
[
Car93
]
.
For the general case, it was showed in
[
Vig16
, Theorem 2.1]
for
\mathds{Z}
-coefficients or
[
Ros15
, Proposition 4.1.1]
for
\mathds{C}
-coefficients.
However, since we have enough ingredients, we prove here using a slightly different argument:

Proof.

(1)

The Braid relation : Let
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
such that
\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})=\ell(ww^{\prime})
, then by Remark
3.7.4
together with Remark
3.11.8
we obtain that

i_{w}^{\flat}*_{I^{\flat}}i_{w^{\prime}}^{\flat}=c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},ww^{\prime})i_{ww^{\prime}}^{\flat}
where
c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},ww^{\prime})=|I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}\cap ww^{\prime}I^{\flat}w^{\prime-1}I|_{{I^{\flat}}}
.
Combining Remark
3.11.9
and Remark
3.7.4
again shows that
c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},ww^{\prime})
must be
1
.

(2)

For the quadratic relations one has by Lemma
3.7.3

I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}\cup I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}
for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
Thus,
(i_{s}^{\flat})^{2}=c^{I^{\flat}}(s,s,1)i_{1}^{\flat}+c^{I^{\flat}}(s,s,s)i_{s}^{\flat}
where
c^{I^{\flat}}(s,s,1)=|I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}|_{I^{\flat}}=q_{s}
and
c^{I^{\flat}}(s,s,s)=|I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\cap sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}|_{I^{\flat}}
by Remark
3.11.8
.

Clearly
sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\subset I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}=I^{\flat}\sqcup I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}
, thus
sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\setminus I^{\flat}\subset I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}
but since
I^{\flat}\cap I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}=\emptyset
we must have

I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\cap sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}=sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\setminus I^{\flat}
, hence
|I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}\cap sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}|_{I^{\flat}}=|sI^{\flat}sI^{\flat}|_{I^{\flat}}-1=|I^{\flat}sI^{\flat}|_{I^{\flat}}-1=q_{s}-1
.∎

Lemma 3.13.2

For any
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
, we have

i_{w}^{\flat}*_{I^{\flat}}i_{w^{\prime}}^{\flat}=i_{ww^{\prime}}^{\flat}\Leftrightarrow I^{\flat}ww^{\prime}I^{\flat}=I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}w^{\prime}I^{\flat}\Leftrightarrow\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})=\ell(ww^{\prime})\Leftrightarrow q_{w}q_{w^{\prime}}=q_{ww^{\prime}}.

Proof.

The first two equivalences can be easily deduced using Remark
3.7.4
and Lemma
3.11.8
. Let us prove the last one.
If
i_{w}^{\flat}*_{I^{\flat}}i_{w^{\prime}}^{\flat}=i_{ww^{\prime}}^{\flat}
, Remark
3.11.9
implies that
q_{w}q_{w^{\prime}}=q_{ww^{\prime}}
.
Now, assume that
q_{w}q_{w^{\prime}}=q_{ww^{\prime}}
, then by Remark
3.11.9
again and Lemma
3.11.8

(c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},ww^{\prime})-1)\int_{G}i_{ww^{\prime}}^{\flat}(a)d\mu_{I^{\flat}}(a)+\sum_{w^{\prime\prime}\in C_{w,w^{\prime}}\setminus\{ww^{\prime}\}}c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime})\int_{G}i_{w^{\prime\prime}}^{\flat}(a)d\mu_{I^{\flat}}(a)=0.

As we saw in the proof of Theorem
3.13.1
, we have
c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},ww^{\prime})=1
.
Accordingly,

\sum_{w^{\prime\prime}\in C_{w,w^{\prime}}\setminus\{ww^{\prime}\}}c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime})\int_{G}i_{w^{\prime\prime}}^{\flat}(a)d\mu_{I^{\flat}}(a)=0.

But if
\{ww^{\prime}\}\subsetneq C_{w,w^{\prime}}
, then all
c^{I^{\flat}}(w,w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime})=\big|I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}\cap w^{\prime\prime}I^{\flat}w^{\prime-1}I^{\flat}\big|_{I^{\flat}}\geq 1
.
This can not be possible, unless
C_{w,w^{\prime}}=\{ww^{\prime}\}
, i.e.
I^{\flat}ww^{\prime}I^{\flat}=I^{\flat}wI^{\flat}w^{\prime}I^{\flat}
, i.e.
i_{w}^{\flat}*_{I^{\flat}}i_{w^{\prime}}^{\flat}=i_{ww^{\prime}}^{\flat}
.
This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The above Iwahori–Matsumoto presentation yields the following consequences:

Corollary 3.13.3

(1)

For any
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
, if
\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})=\ell(ww^{\prime})
then

I_{w^{-1}(\mathfrak{a})}I\cap I_{w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{a})}I=(I_{w^{-1}(\mathfrak{a})}\cap I_{w^{\prime}(\mathfrak{a})})I=I
.

(2)

The
\mathds{Z}
-linear map
z\mapsto i_{z}^{\flat}
embeds the group algebra
\mathds{Z}[\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}]
into
\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})
.

(3)

For all
w\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
and
z\in\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}
, one has
i_{w}^{\flat}i_{z}^{\flat}=i_{z}^{\flat}i_{z^{-1}wz}^{\flat}
.

(4)

Let
w\in W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
and
w=s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{\ell(w)}
a reduced expression.
Using Lemma
3.7.3
, one has

q_{w}=q_{s_{1}}q_{s_{2}}\cdots q_{s_{\ell(w)}}.

(1)

(5)

Let
w=w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\cdot z\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
with
w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}=s_{1}\cdots s_{\ell(w)}
a reduced word in
W_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
and
z\in\widetilde{\Omega}^{\flat}
, we have

\displaystyle(i_{w}^{\flat})^{-1}=i_{z^{-1}}^{\flat}(i_{w_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}}^{\flat})^{-1}=q_{w}^{-1}{i_{z^{-1}}^{\flat}(i_{s_{\ell(w)}}^{\flat}-q_{s_{\ell(w)}}+1)\cdots(i_{s_{1}}^{\flat}-q_{s_{1}}+1)}.

which lives in
\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z}[q^{-1}])
given Lemma
3.8.2
.

(6)

If we do not include
q^{-1}
in the coefficients ring, we still have

i_{w}^{\flat}(i_{w}^{\flat})^{*}=(i_{w}^{\flat})^{*}i_{w}^{\flat}=q_{w},\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z}),

where,
(i_{w}^{\flat})^{*}:=(i_{s_{\ell(w)}}^{\flat}-q_{s_{\ell(w)}}+1)\cdots(i_{s_{1}}^{\flat}-q_{s_{1}}+1)
.

(7)

For any
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
, set
{P}_{s}^{l}:=\{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\colon w<sw,\,i.e.\,\ell(sw)=\ell(w)+1\}
and
{P}_{s}^{r}:=\{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}\colon w>ws,\,i.e.\,\ell(ws)=\ell(w)-1\}
then
({P}_{s}^{l})^{-1}={P}_{s}^{r}

and

\widetilde{W}={P}_{s}^{l}\sqcup s{P}_{s}^{l}={P}_{s}^{r}\sqcup{P}_{s}^{r}s

[
Bou68
, Ch. IV, §1.7]
.
For any
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\flat}
and any
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}
, we have

i_{s}^{\flat}*_{{I^{\flat}}}i_{w}^{\flat}=\begin{cases}i_{sw}^{\flat},&\text{ if }w\in{P}_{s}^{l},\\
\hskip 0.0ptq_{s}i_{w}^{\flat}+(q_{s}-1)i_{sw}^{\flat},&\text{ if }w\in s{P}_{s}^{l}.\end{cases}\text{ and }i_{w}^{\flat}*_{{I^{\flat}}}i_{s}^{\flat}=\begin{cases}i_{ws}^{\flat},&\text{ if }w\in{P}_{s}^{r},\\
\hskip 0.0ptq_{s}i_{w}^{\flat}+(q_{s}-1)i_{ws}^{\flat},&\text{ if }w\in{P}_{s}^{r}s.\end{cases}

Proof.

These properties follow readily from Theorem
3.13.1
.∎

Proposition 3.13.4

Set
\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z}):=\mathcal{H}(G_{1}^{\flat}\sslash I^{\flat},\mathds{Z})
and let
\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}^{\Upomega}(\mathds{Z})
be the subalgebra of functions supported on cosets of the form
\sigma I^{\flat}
for
\sigma\in\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}
.
We have an isomorphism of
\mathds{Z}
-algebras

\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})\simeq\mathds{Z}[\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}]\otimes^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z})

where,
\otimes^{\prime}
indicates that the tensor modules is endowed with a ”twisted” multiplication: for any
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}
and any
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\flat}

(\sigma\otimes i_{w})(\sigma^{\prime}\otimes i_{w^{\prime}})=\sigma\sigma^{\prime}\otimes i_{\sigma^{\prime-1}w\sigma^{\prime}}*_{I^{\flat}}i_{w^{\prime}}.

Proof.

This an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.13.1
and Lemma
3.3.1
together with
2
and
3
of Corollary
3.13.3
.
∎

Remark 3.13.5
.

The restriction of the surjective morphism (Corollary
3.12.2
) of
\iota^{\flat}\colon\mathcal{H}_{I}({\mathds{Z}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}({\mathds{Z}})
to
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z})
is injective (Remark
3.3.4
) and yields an isomorphism of algebras
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z})
since
G_{1}^{\flat}\sslash I^{\flat}\simeq G_{1}\sslash I
.
Thanks to Remark
3.3.4
, the following diagram is commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{I}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{Z}[\widetilde{\Upomega}]\otimes^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{I}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}(\mathds{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{Z}[\widetilde{\Upomega}^{\flat}]\otimes^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{I^{\flat}}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square^{\flat}\otimes\iota^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

3.14.
Root data

Recall that we have defined in §
2.5
the root system
\Sigma
and that we have
\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\vee}=\mathds{Z}[\Sigma]
.
Set
\Sigma^{\flat}\subset\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
for the subset corresponding to
\Sigma
under the isomorphism
\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}\simeq\Lambda_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}
(§
3.1
).
We consider the root datum
\mathcal{D}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat}:=({\Lambda}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat},\Sigma^{\flat,\vee},{\Lambda}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}^{\flat,\vee},\Sigma^{\flat})\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}
.

Define the free abelian group
\Lambda_{M}^{1,\vee}\colon=\{x\in X_{*}(\mathbf{S})\otimes_{\mathds{Z}}\mathds{R}\colon\langle x,\nu_{M}(\Lambda_{M}^{1})\rangle_{\mathds{R}}\subset\mathds{Z}\}
.
We obtain in this way a perfect pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon\Lambda_{M}^{1}\times\Lambda_{M}^{1,\vee}\to\mathds{Z}
(§
2.2
).
Therefore, we have in our hands another root datum

\mathcal{D}:=({\Lambda}_{M}^{1},\Sigma^{1,\vee},{\Lambda}_{M}^{1,\vee},\Sigma^{1},\Delta^{1,\vee}).

In
[
Lus89
, §3.2]
, Lusztig attaches to any quadruplet
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathbf{W},v=?,L)
(where,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is a root datum,
\mathbf{W}
the extended affine group associated with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,
v
an indeterminate §3.2 in
loc. cit.
and
L\colon\mathbf{W}\to\mathds{N}
a function §3.1 in
loc. cit.
verifying an additivity condition) a Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathbf{W},v=?,L)
generated by elements
\{T_{w}\colon w\in\mathbf{W}\}
, denoted by
H
in
loc. cit.
.

Corollary 3.14.1

The map
i_{w}\mapsto T_{w}
yields two isomorphisms of Hecke algebras

\mathcal{H}_{{I}^{1}}(\mathds{Z}[q^{-1}])\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.11113pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D},\widetilde{W}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},v=q,L)\text{ and }\mathcal{H}_{{I}}^{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}}(\mathds{Z}[q^{-1}])\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.11113pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},{W}_{\textit{\emph{\text{aff}}}},v=q,L)

where,
L\colon W\to\mathbb{N},w\mapsto-\frac{1}{2}\omega(q_{w})
.

Proof.

The corollary follows readily from the Iwahori–Matsumoto presentation in Theorem
3.13.1
and Remark
3.13.5
, because
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathbf{W},v=?,L)
is characterized precisely by the same Braid relation
[
Lus89
, §2.1]
and quadratic relations
[
Lus89
, §3.2]
.
∎

3.15.
Properties of dominance
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}

Lemma 3.15.1

We have the following properties regarding
\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
:

(1)

If
m_{1},m_{2},\dots,m_{k}\in\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
, then there exists
m_{\circ}\in\Lambda_{M}^{-\flat}
such that
m_{\circ}+m_{{I^{\flat}}}\in\Lambda_{M}^{-,\flat}
12
12

12

We will denote the operation on
\Lambda_{M}
additively.
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.

(2)

If
m_{1},m_{2}\in\Lambda_{M}^{-,\flat}
, then
\ell(m_{1}+m_{2})=\ell(m_{1})+\ell(m_{2})
.

For any
w\in{W}
, we have:

(3)

\ell(mw)=\ell(m)+\ell(w)
if
m\in\Lambda_{M}^{-,\flat}
and
\ell(wm)=\ell(w)+\ell(m)
if
m\in\Lambda_{M}^{+,\flat}
.

(4)

\ell(w(m))=\ell(wmw^{-1})=\ell(m)
for all
m\in\Lambda_{M}^{\flat}
.

Proof.
